CERN-TH-2026-xxx, DESY-26-013 Heavy-quark production in deep-inelastic scattering – Mellin moments of structure functions –
Marco Klann, Sven-Olaf Moch, Kay Schönwald[3ex] (a) II. Institut für Theoretische Physik, Universität Hamburg, D-22761 Hamburg, Germany(b) Theoretical Physics Department, CERN, 1211 Geneva 23, Switzerland

Abstract
We compute Mellin moments of the heavy‑quark structure functions in deep-inelastic scattering at next‑to‑leading order in quantum chromodynamics, retaining their full dependence on the heavy‑quark mass. Using the optical theorem and the operator product expansion, we derive analytic results for fixed Mellin moments N = 2 N=2 to 22 of the structure functions F 2 F_{2} and F L F_{L} . Our results reproduce the known expressions in the relevant asymptotic limits, in particular for virtualities of the exchanged photon Q 2 Q^{2} much larger than the heavy‑quark mass squared m 2 m^{2} , and are in agreement with existing parametrisations of the next‑to‑leading‑order coefficient functions. The computational set‑up developed in this work also provides a direct pathway toward extending these calculations to next‑to‑next‑to‑leading order.



1
Introduction

Heavy‑quark production in deep‑inelastic scattering (DIS) plays a central role in testing quantum chromodynamics (QCD) and in determining parton distribution functions (PDFs) at high energies.
In neutral‑current (NC) DIS, heavy quarks such as charm or bottom are produced predominantly through photon–gluon fusion, in which a highly virtual photon with momentum
q
interacts with a gluon inside the nucleon.
The presence of the heavy‑quark mass
m
introduces a hierarchy of scales, making these processes a sensitive probe of perturbative QCD.
Measurements of charm and bottom production at the HERA collider (see Ref.
[
12
]
) have established heavy‑quark DIS as an essential component of precision QCD phenomenology and have provided stringent constraints on the gluon PDF down to small values of the Bjorken scaling variable
x
.
Further high‑precision data are anticipated from the future Electron–Ion Collider (EIC) (see Ref.
[
1
]
), which will explore a similar kinematic range while significantly deepening the experimental understanding of heavy‑flavour production.

The cross section for unpolarised heavy-quark production in DIS for NC exchange by photons of virtuality
Q^{2}=-q^{2}>0
is expressed in terms of the heavy-flavour structure functions
F_{k}(x,Q^{2},m^{2})
with
k=2,L
.
Standard collinear QCD factorisation expresses the structure functions as convolutions of the PDFs
f_{i}
with the corresponding coefficient functions
C_{k,i}
.
The latter depend on the renormalisation scale
\mu_{r}
and the factorisation scale
\mu_{f}
, which control the running of the strong coupling and the separation between long‑ and short‑distance physics, respectively. Thus,

F_{k}(x,Q^{2},m^{2})\;=\;\sum\limits_{i\,\in\,\{{\rm q,{\bar{q}},g}\}}\,\,\int_{x}^{\,z^{\,\rm max}}{dz\over z}\>f_{i}\left({x\over z},\,\mu_{f}^{2}\right)\,C_{k,i}\left(z,\,Q^{2},\,m^{2},\,\mu_{f}^{2},\,\mu_{r}^{2}\right)\;,

(1)

where
z
is the parton momentum fraction bounded by
z^{\,\rm max}=1/(1+4\>\!m^{2}/Q^{2})
.
The partonic kinematic variables (including the partonic centre-of-mass energy
s
)
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle s=Q^{2}\left(\frac{1}{z}-1\right)\,,\quad\xi=\frac{Q^{2}}{m^{2}}\,,\quad\eta=\frac{s}{4m^{2}}-1\,,

(2)

see for instance Ref.
[
79
]
.
In this approach, the heavy quarks appear only as final‑state particles, and leading‑twist factorization ensures that the kinematic conditions for intrinsic charm are not satisfied (see e.g., Ref.
[
30
]
).

QCD perturbation theory provides a systematic expansion of observables in powers of the strong coupling
\alpha_{s}
, allowing the DIS coefficient functions in Eq. (
1
) to be computed with high precision.
In the heavy‑quark sector, this requires retaining the full dependence on the heavy‑quark mass, and the complete QCD corrections to these coefficient functions are known at next‑to‑leading order (NLO) in QCD in numerical form; see Refs.
[
65
,
79
,
55
]
.
These results have also been used to construct a parametrization in Mellin space; see Ref.
[
14
]
.
Analytic results at NLO have first been presented in the limit
Q^{2}\gg m^{2}
(see Ref.
[
35
]
)
followed by Refs.
[
27
,
24
]
, which contain the complete results with full mass dependence for the quark-flavour non-singlet and pure-singlet coefficient functions in Eq. (
1
).
Analytic results with full mass dependence for the NLO gluon coefficient functions
C_{k,{\rm g}}

are unknown.

Current global PDF fits (see, e.g. Ref.
[
13
]
) show that the precision of heavy‑flavour measurements from HERA, together with the anticipated accuracy of future EIC data, requires substantially improved theoretical predictions.
The experimental uncertainties are now small enough that NLO accuracy no longer matches the available precision, especially in regions sensitive to the small‑
x
gluon PDFs.
To fully exploit these data, next‑to‑next‑to‑leading‑order (NNLO) QCD calculations are required.
At NNLO accuracy there are now complete results in the limit
Q^{2}\gg m^{2}
available (see Refs.
[
7
,
8
]
building on Refs.
[
11
,
10
,
19
,
3
,
9
,
4
,
21
,
25
,
2
,
5
,
6
,
20
]
), which can be combined with other partial information on the coefficient functions in the threshold region
s\simeq 4\>\!m^{2}
,
cf. Refs.
[
64
,
60
]
and in
the high-energy regime
s\gg m^{2}
, cf. Ref.
[
37
]
.

We aim to extend the heavy‑quark DIS coefficient functions to NNLO, and as a first step toward this goal we compute Mellin moments of the DIS structure functions while retaining full heavy‑quark mass dependence.
To set up the formalism, we report here on the calculation of these moments at NLO and confirm agreement with existing results.
In particular, this allows us to verify the accuracy of the parametrisations of the NLO coefficient functions provided in Ref.
[
79
]
.

The article is organised as follows. In Sec.
2
we set the stage and lay the groundwork for the calculation of the heavy-quark DIS coefficient functions at NLO in QCD.
In Sec.
3
we compute their Mellin moments for fixed values of
N
using the harmonic projection and an expansion of the forward Compton amplitude in the external momenta.
This approach encounters bottlenecks for large
N
in the integral reductions, so that we also adopt an alternative method, performing the integral reductions without relying on a momentum expansion.
Sec.
4
presents the results and discusses the various checks performed.
We conclude in Sec.
5
with an outlook on the next steps toward NNLO accuracy.
Appendix
A
provides details on the flavour factors relevant for the heavy‑quark structure functions.

2
Set-up

2.1
Hadronic Tensor

This section and the subsequent ones lay the groundwork for the calculation of fixed Mellin moments of the coefficient functions.
It closely follows the presentation in Refs.
[
75
,
68
]
.
For a more detailed review on the hadronic tensor, operator product expansion and renormalisation the reader is referred to Ref.
[
34
]
.
To this end, the hadronic part of lepton-hadron scattering is considered, allowing for both light-quark fields
\psi_{\mathrm{l}}
and heavy-quark fields
\psi_{\mathrm{h}}
.
These fields are coupled to external vector bosons, such as the photon, and the corresponding interactions are determined by a locally conserved current,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{J^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{J^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{J^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{J^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}=\bar{\psi}_{\text{l}}Q^{\text{v}}_{\text{l}}\mathchoice{\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\psi_{\text{l}}+\bar{\psi}_{\text{h}}Q^{\text{v}}_{\text{h}}\mathchoice{\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\psi_{\text{h}}\,,

(3)

where
Q^{\text{v}}_{\text{l}}
and
Q^{\text{v}}_{\text{h}}
denote the charge matrices for light- and heavy-quark flavours, respectively.

In the context of deep-inelastic scattering, the focus is typically on unpolarised hadronic states with momentum
p
and spin
s
, representing particles such as the proton.
The spin-averaged hadronic matrix element of a product of vector currents is given by

\displaystyle\bra{p,\text{hadron} }\mathchoice{J^{{{\dagger}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{J^{{{\dagger}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{J^{{{\dagger}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{J^{{{\dagger}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}(z)\mathchoice{J^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{J^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{J^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.0 }\,.

(4)

Here,
\mathcal{S}
denotes the set of all possible spin states of the hadron and

\absolutevalue{\mathcal{S}}
is its cardinality.
The hadronic tensor is the Fourier transform of the spin-averaged matrix element

\displaystyle\mathchoice{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}(p,q)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}z\,e^{iq\cdot z}\bra{p}\mathchoice{J^{{{\dagger}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{J^{{{\dagger}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{J^{{{\dagger}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{J^{{{\dagger}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}(z)\mathchoice{J^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{J^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{J^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\mak (5)

It should be noted that the hadronic tensor also depends on the heavy-quark mass
m
and the strong coupling
\alpha_{s}
.
By convention, however, functional dependencies arising from parameters of the Lagrangian are suppressed unless they play an essential role in the discussion.

The hadronic tensor is constrained by Lorentz symmetry, parity, time-reversal invariance, and current conservation, and can therefore be written as

\displaystyle\mathchoice{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}(p,q)=\frac{1}{2x}\mathchoice{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.862 {$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{d^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}(p,q)F_{2}(p,q)\,,

(6)

where the
F_{i}
denote the structure functions, accompanied by symmetric and transverse tensors

(7b)

2.2
Forward Scattering Amplitude

The exponential in the hadronic tensor becomes highly oscillatory in the deep-inelastic limit
-q^{2}\to\infty
.
By the Riemann-Lebesgue lemma (see Refs.
[
80
,
76
]
), the hadronic tensor is then dominated by contributions on the light cone,
z^{2}=0
, which is also referred to as light-cone dominance (see Refs.
[
57
,
32
]
).

The hadronic tensor can be related to the discontinuity of the forward scattering amplitude.
Assuming that the momentum transfer
q
is space-like and that the hadronic tensor is sufficiently well-behaved for the Schwarz reflection principle (or suitable generalisations) to hold, it can be expressed in terms of the imaginary part of the forward scattering amplitude.
For a modern account of the relation between imaginary parts and discontinuities, the reader is referred to Ref.
[
53
]
.
This relation is an instance of the optical theorem

\displaystyle\mathchoice{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}(p,q)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\imaginary\,\mathchoice{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}(p,q)\,,

(8)

where the forward scattering amplitude is defined (using time-ordering
\mathcal{T}
) by

\displaystyle\mathchoice{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}(p,q)=i\int\mathrm{d}^{4}z\,e^{iq\cdot z}\bra{p}\mathcal{T}\{\mathchoice{J^{{{\dagger}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{J^{{{\dagger}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{J^{{{\dagger}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{J^{{{\dagger}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}(z)\mathchoice{J^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{J^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{J^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{J^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}(0)\}\ket{p}\,.

(9)

Through the optical theorem, the hadronic tensor is directly connected to the operator product expansion of the forward scattering amplitude.
At leading twist, the forward amplitude admits an expansion in terms of local operators, with the tensor structure once more constrained by Lorentz symmetry, parity, and time-reversal invariance,

Here, braces denote a completely symmetric and traceless combination of indices.
The outer sum runs over all spin values, while the inner sum iterates over all local, symmetric, traceless, twist-two operators of a given spin.
More concretely, these operators are divided into quark non-singlet, quark singlet, and gluon operators

(11c)

The
\lambda^{a}_{l}
denote the generators of the global flavour symmetry group, and
D^{\mu}
represents the ordinary gauge-covariant derivative.
Since hadrons are described within the parton model, where only massless partonic degrees of freedom are considered, the quark operators are restricted to the light-flavour sector and contributions from heavy-quark operators are omitted at this stage.

Under the assumption that the squared bosonic momentum transfer is much larger than the hadron mass squared, hadronic mass effects in the operator matrix element may be neglected.
In the leading-twist approximation, this gives

\displaystyle\bra{p}\mathchoice{O^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{\lambda_{1}}{\dots}{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{O^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{\lambda_{1}}{\dots}{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{O^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{\lambda_{1}}{\dots}{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebo \displaystyle=\mathchoice{p^{{{\{\lambda_{1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\{\lambda_{1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\{\lambda_{1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\{\lambda_{1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\dots\mathchoice{p^{{{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{N}_{k,}(p)

(12)

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{p^{{{\phantom{\{}\lambda_{1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\phantom{\{}\lambda_{1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\phantom{\{}\lambda_{1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\phantom{\{}\lambda_{1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\dots\mathchoice{p^{{{\phantom{\{}\lambda_{N}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\phantom{\{}\lambda_{N}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\phantom{\{}\lambda_{N}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\phantom{\{}\lambda_{N}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{N}_{k,}(p)+\mathcal{O}\left(p^{2}\right)\,.

These operator matrix elements are not accessible within perturbative QCD, but this is not an obstacle, as the primary interest lies in the coefficients of the operator product expansion of the forward scattering amplitude.
Since the expansion is valid at the operator level, these coefficients are universal and independent of the external states.
Further details are provided in the discussion of renormalisation in the subsequent section.

Upon substituting the operator matrix elements into the operator product expansion of the forward scattering amplitude and performing some basic algebraic manipulations, one finds

\displaystyle\mathchoice{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}(p,q)

\displaystyle=\sum_{N}\sum_{k\in\set{a,\text{q},\text{g}}}\left(\frac{1}{x}\right)^{N}A^{N}_{k,}(p)\Bigg[\mathchoice{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}C^{N}_{L,k}(q)+\mathchoice{d^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{d^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{d^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{d^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}C^{N}_{2,k}(q)\Bigg]+\mathcal{O}\left(p^{2}\right)\,.

(13)

In the leading twist approximation, the forward scattering amplitude may be decomposed in direct analogy to the hadronic tensor,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}(p,q)=\mathchoice{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}(p,q)T_{L}(p,q)+\mathchoice{d^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{d^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{d^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{d^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}(p,q)T_{2}(p,q)+\mathcal{O}(p^{2})\,.

(14)

The hadronic invariants are defined as follows

\displaystyle T_{k}(p,q)

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\mathcal{P}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.46649pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.46649pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.78854pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84894pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{\text{S},k}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{P}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.46649pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.46649pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.78854pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84894pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{\text{S},k}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{P}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.46649pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.46649pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.78854pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84894pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{\text{S},k}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{P}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.46649pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.46649pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.78854pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84894pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{\text{S},k}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(p,q)\mathchoice{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}(p,q)\,,\qquad k\in\{2,L\}\,,

(15a)

where the projectors are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{S},L}^{\mu\nu}(p,q)

\displaystyle=-\frac{q^{2}}{\left(p\cdot q\right)^{2}}\mathchoice{p^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{p^{{{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\,,

(16a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{S},2}^{\mu\nu}(p,q)

\displaystyle=-\frac{(D-1)}{(D-2)}\frac{q^{2}}{\left(p\cdot q\right)^{2}}\mathchoice{p^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{p^{{{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}-\frac{1}{(D-2)}\mathchoice{g^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\,.

(16b)

Having expressed the forward scattering amplitude in terms of the same tensor structures as the hadronic tensor, the hadronic invariants are directly related to the structure functions via the optical theorem,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\imaginary T_{i}(p,q)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2x}F_{k}(p,q)\,,\qquad i\in\set{2,L}\,.

(17)

By exploiting the invariance of the forward scattering amplitude for vector–vector scattering under crossing of the external bosons, and by applying the inverse Mellin transform of the operator product expansion together with the optical theorem and crossing symmetry (see Ref.
[
33
]
), the hadronic invariants can be related to the structure functions

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\differential{x}x^{N-2}F_{i}(p,q)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\set{a,\text{q},\text{g}}}A^{N}_{k,}(p)C^{N}_{i,k}(q)\,,\quad i\in\set{2,L}\,.

(18)

Note that the above relation fixes only the even moments of the structure functions. However, by analytic continuation, all moments in the complex
N
-plane are determined, provided the even moments are known.

2.3
Renormalisation

Since the goal is to determine fixed Mellin moments of the coefficient functions from the hadronic invariants, the only remaining complication stems from the hadronic operator matrix elements.
As the operator product expansion of the forward scattering amplitude is valid at the operator level, the coefficient functions are independent of the choice of external states, with all hadronic information encoded solely in the operator matrix elements.

This allows the hadronic states to be replaced by partonic ones carrying momentum
p
, since partons are taken to be massless in contrast to hadrons.
The relevant partonic states consist of non-singlet and singlet quark configurations, as well as gluons, with the corresponding operator matrix elements given by

\displaystyle\bra{p,i}\mathchoice{\mathcal{O}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{\lambda_{1}}{\dots}{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{O}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{\lambda_{1}}{\dots}{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{O}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{\lambda_{1}}{\dots}{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{O}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{\lambda_{1}}{\dots}{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\ket{p,i}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{p^{{{\{\lambda_{1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\{\lambda_{1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\{\lambda_{1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\{\lambda_{1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\dots\mathchoice{p^{{{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{N}_{k,i}(p)\,,\qquad k\in\set{a,\text{q},\text{g}}\,,

(19)

which, in general, are divergent and require renormalisation, see Ref.
[
43
]
for more details.
To regularise the singularities, dimensional regularisation (see Refs.
[
96
,
31
,
15
,
42
]
) is employed in
D=4-2\epsilon
space–time dimensions.
In this framework, the bare operators are related to the renormalised ones through

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathcal{O}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{\lambda_{1}}{\dots}{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{O}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{\lambda_{1}}{\dots}{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{O}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{\lambda_{1}}{\dots}{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{O}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{\lambda_{1}}{\dots}{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\{\text{q},\text{g}\}}Z_{kl}\mathchoice{\mathcal{O}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.78134pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.78134pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.8855pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.77533pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{\lambda_{1}}{\dots}{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{{k,\text{ren}}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{O}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.78134pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.78134pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.8855pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.77533pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{\lambda_{1}}{\dots}{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{{k,\text{ren}}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{O}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.78134pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.78134pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.8855pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.77533pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{\lambda_{1}}{\dots}{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{{k,\text{ren}}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{O}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.78134pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.78134pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.8855pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.77533pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{\lambda_{1}}{\dots}{\lambda_{N}\}}}}_{{{k,\text{ren}}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.69308pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.10835pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.29169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.71432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.5521pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.73543pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\,,\qquad k\in\set{\text{q},\text{g}}\,,

(20)

where the renormalisation constants
Z_{kl}
generally induce mixing among the singlet operators.
The scale dependence of the renormalised operators is then governed by the renormalisation group equation,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\log\mu^{2}}\mathcal{O}_{l}^{\{\lambda_{1}\dots\lambda_{N}\}}

(21)

\displaystyle=\derivative{\log\mu^2 }\sum_{k\in\set{\text{q},\text{g}}}\left(Z^{-1}\right)_{lk}\mathcal{O}_{k,\text{ren}}^{\{\lambda_{1}\dots\lambda_{N}\}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\set{\text{q},\text{g}}}\left[\derivative{\left(Z^{-1}\right)_{lk}}{\log\mu^2 }\mathcal{O}_{k,\text{ren}}^{\{\lambda_{1}\dots\lambda_{N}\}}+\left(Z^{-1}\right)_{lk}\derivative{\mathcal{O}_{k, \ren}^{\{\lambda_1\dots\lambda_N\}}}{\log\mu^2 }\right]

\displaystyle\equiv-\gamma_{lk}\mathcal{O}_{k,\text{ren}}^{\{\lambda_{1}\dots\lambda_{N}\}}+\derivative{\mathcal{O}_{l, \ren}^{\{\lambda_1\dots\lambda_N\}}}{\log\mu^2 }\,.

Here, the fact that bare operators are independent of the renormalisation scale has been used, together with the relation

\displaystyle\delta_{lk}=\sum_{j\in\set{\text{g},\text{q}}}Z_{lj}\left(Z^{-1}\right)_{jk}\;\Rightarrow\;0=\sum_{j\in\set{\text{g},\text{q}}}Z_{lj}\derivative{\left(Z^{-1} \right)_{jk}}{\log\mu^2 }+\sum_{j\in\set{\text{g},\text{q}}}\derivative{Z_{lj}}{\log\mu^2 }\left(Z^{-1}\right)_{jk}\,.

(22)

The renormalisation constants and the implicitly defined anomalous dimensions
\gamma
are, in general, matrix–valued.
Since the
Z_{ij}
depend on the renormalisation scale
\mu^{2}
only implicitly, one finds from the chain rule that

\displaystyle\gamma_{lk}=-\sum_{j\in\set{\text{g},\text{q}}}\left(-\epsilon a_{s,\text{ren}}+\beta\right)\partialderivative{Z_{lj} }{a_{s,\ren}}\left(Z^{-1}\right)_{jk}\,,

(23)

with the QCD beta function and the rescaled coupling,

\displaystyle a_{s,\text{ren}}=\frac{\alpha_{s,\text{ren}}}{4\pi}\,.

(24)

All quanitites, the renormalisation constants, the anomalous dimensions, and the beta function admit power–series expansions in the strong coupling.
In addition, the renormalisation constants and anomalous dimensions also expand in the dimensional regulator
\epsilon
.
No positive powers of the regulator are assumed to occur in either expansion, since such terms amount to finite renormalisations.
This assumption is equivalent to working in the modified minimal–subtraction scheme (see Refs.
[
16
,
97
,
93
]
).
Because the anomalous dimensions are finite quantities, this choice of scheme reduces their expansion to a power series in the coupling only.
In this scheme, the expansions read

\displaystyle Z_{ij}

\displaystyle=Z^{(0,0)}_{ij}+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{n=1}^{m}Z^{(m,n)}_{ij}\frac{a_{s,\text{ren}}^{m}}{\epsilon^{n}}\,,

(25a)

\displaystyle\gamma_{ij}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{(m)}_{ij}a_{s,\text{ren}}^{m}\,,

(25b)

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle=-\sum_{m=2}^{\infty}\beta_{m-2}a_{s,\text{ren}}^{m}\,.

(25c)

For our calculation, we require the Mellin moments of the anomalous dimensions up to two loops (see Refs.
[
52
,
46
,
68
,
75
]
) .
Similarly, the coefficients of the beta function
\beta_{m}
are needed only up to two loops (see Refs.
[
58
,
36
,
45
]
).

At zeroth order in the coupling, the operators are finite and do not mix, so no renormalisation is required.
Accordingly, the
Z
–factors may be chosen diagonal and normalised such that

\displaystyle Z_{ij}=\delta_{ij}+\mathcal{O}\left(\alpha_{s,\text{ren}}\right)\,.

(26)

With this normalisation, the renormalisation constants are fully determined by the anomalous dimensions and the beta function coefficients, allowing a recursive solution order by order in the strong coupling and the dimensional regulator
\epsilon
. One finds

\displaystyle Z_{\text{qq}}

\displaystyle=1+a_{s,\text{ren}}\frac{\gamma_{\text{qq}}^{(0)}}{\epsilon}+a_{s,\text{ren}}^{2}\Bigg(\frac{-{\beta_{0}}\gamma_{\text{qq}}^{(0)}+\gamma_{\text{gq}}^{(0)}\gamma_{\text{qg}}^{(0)}+\left(\gamma_{\text{qq}}^{(0)}\right)^{2}}{2\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{\gamma_{\text{qq}}^{(1)}}{2\epsilon}\Bigg)+\mathcal{O}(a_{s,\text{ren}}^{3})

(27a)

\displaystyle Z_{\text{qg}}

\displaystyle=a_{s,\text{ren}}\frac{\gamma_{\text{qg}}^{(0)}}{\epsilon}+a_{s,\text{ren}}^{2}\Bigg(\frac{-{\beta_{0}}\gamma_{\text{qg}}^{(0)}+\gamma_{\text{gg}}^{(0)}\gamma_{\text{qg}}^{(0)}+\gamma_{\text{qq}}^{(0)}\gamma_{\text{qg}}^{(0)}}{2\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{\gamma_{\text{qg}}^{(1)}}{2\epsilon}\Bigg)+\mathcal{O}(a_{s,\text{ren}}^{3})

(27b)

\displaystyle Z_{\text{gq}}

\displaystyle=a_{s,\text{ren}}\frac{\gamma_{\text{gq}}^{(0)}}{\epsilon}+a_{s,\text{ren}}^{2}\Bigg(\frac{-{\beta_{0}}\gamma_{\text{gq}}^{(0)}+\gamma_{\text{gg}}^{(0)}\gamma_{\text{gq}}^{(0)}+\gamma_{\text{qq}}^{(0)}\gamma_{\text{gq}}^{(0)}}{2\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{\gamma_{\text{gq}}^{(1)}}{2\epsilon}\Bigg)+\mathcal{O}(a_{s,\text{ren}}^{3})

(27c)

\displaystyle Z_{\text{gg}}

\displaystyle=1+a_{s,\text{ren}}\frac{\gamma_{\text{gg}}^{(0)}}{\epsilon}+a_{s,\text{ren}}^{2}\Bigg(\frac{-{\beta_{0}}\gamma_{\text{gg}}^{(0)}+\left(\gamma_{\text{gg}}^{(0)}\right)^{2}+\gamma_{\text{gq}}^{(0)}\gamma_{\text{qg}}^{(0)}}{2\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{\gamma_{\text{gg}}^{(1)}}{2\epsilon}\Bigg)+\mathcal{O}(a_{s,\text{ren}}^{3})\,.

(27d)

Finally, to renormalise the operator product expansion of the forward amplitude, we replace the bare operators with their renormalised counterparts and, consequently, the operator matrix elements with the renormalised ones.
In the leading-twist approximation, this yields the renormalised partonic invariants,

\displaystyle T_{i,\text{p}}(p,q)

\displaystyle=\sum_{N}\left(\frac{1}{x}\right)^{N}\sum_{j,k}C_{i,j}^{N}Z_{jk}A^{\text{N,\text{ren}}}_{\text{p},\text{k}}(p)\,,\qquad i\in\set{L,2}\,.

(28)

This completes the renormalisation of the right-hand side of the forward scattering amplitude, namely its operator product expansion.

To determine the coefficient functions, the forward scattering amplitude must also be computed directly from Feynman diagrams.
These amplitudes require renormalisation as well; throughout the following, the minimal subtraction scheme will be used.

The first step is the renormalisation of the coupling, performed in a theory with
n_{\text{l}}+n_{\text{h}}
active flavours, with
a_{s,\text{ren}}^{(n_{\text{l}}+n_{\text{h}})}
denoting the corresponding coupling,

\displaystyle\hat{a}_{s,\text{ren}}

\displaystyle=a_{s,\text{ren}}^{(n_{\text{l}}+n_{\text{h}})}\left[1-a_{s,\text{ren}}^{(n_{\text{l}}+n_{\text{h}})}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left(\frac{11}{3}C_{A}-\frac{4}{3}T_{F}(n_{\text{l}}+n_{\text{h}})\right)+\mathcal{O}\left((a_{s,\text{ren}}^{(n_{\text{l}}+n_{\text{h}})})^{2}\right)\right]\,.

(29)

However, since we perform mass factorisation only with
n_{l}
active flavours, this discrepancy must be accounted for by the decoupling relation (see Ref.
[
40
]
),

\displaystyle a_{s,\text{ren}}^{(n_{\text{l}}+n_{\text{h}})}

\displaystyle=a_{s,\text{ren}}\bigg\{1+a_{s,\text{ren}}n_{\text{h}}T_{F}\bigg[\frac{4}{3}\log\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{m^{2}}\right)+\frac{2}{3}\epsilon\bigg(\log^{2}\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{m^{2}}\right)+\zeta_{2}\bigg)

(30)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon^{2}\right)\bigg]+\mathcal{O}\left(a_{s,\text{ren}}^{2}\right)\bigg\}\,.

For the field renormalisation, it is noted that the vector boson and scalar wave functions require no renormalisation, since QCD corrections to their propagators appear only at next-to-leading order in the respective couplings and are neglected at leading order,

\displaystyle Z_{\text{v}}

\displaystyle=1+\mathcal{O}\left(g_{\text{v}}^{2}\right)\,.

(31)

In contrast, gluon and light-quark propagators receive QCD corrections from heavy-quark loops, at one loop for gluons and at two loops for light quarks, making their wave, function renormalisation (see Refs.
[
39
,
23
]
) necessary.

\displaystyle Z_{2}

\displaystyle=1+\hat{a}_{s,\text{ren}}^{2}\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{m^{2}}\right)^{2\epsilon}C_{F}T_{F}n_{\text{h}}\left[\frac{1}{\epsilon}-\frac{5}{6}+\epsilon\left(\frac{89}{36}+\zeta_{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\right]+\mathcal{O}\left(\hat{a}_{s,\text{ren}}^{3}\right)\,,

(32a)

\displaystyle Z_{3}

\displaystyle=1+\hat{a}_{s,\text{ren}}\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{m^{2}}\right)^{\epsilon}T_{F}n_{\text{h}}\left[-\frac{4}{3\epsilon}-\epsilon\frac{2}{3}\zeta_{2}+\epsilon^{2}\frac{4}{9}\zeta_{3}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{3})\right]+\mathcal{O}\left(\hat{a}_{s,\text{ren}}^{2}\right)\,.

(32b)

Moreover, since internal quarks are allowed to carry a mass, this mass must likewise be renormalised, which is accomplished via counterterm insertions,

\displaystyle\delta m=\hat{a}_{s,\text{ren}}\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{m^{2}}\right)^{\epsilon}\left[-\frac{3}{\epsilon}-4-\epsilon\left(8+\frac{3}{2}\zeta_{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\right]+\mathcal{O}\left(\hat{a}_{s,\text{ren}}^{2}\right)\,.

(33)

3
Mellin moments for fixed values of
N

3.1
Harmonic Tensors

We now introduce the harmonic tensors that form the foundation for harmonic projection, which we employ to extract the Mellin moments of the coefficient functions.
Harmonic tensors of rank
N
, constructed from a reference vector
q_{\mu}
and the metric tensor
g_{\mu\nu}
, are defined as symmetric and traceless tensors, normalised such that

\displaystyle=q^{2}\mathchoice{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu_{1}}{\ldots}{\hat{\mu}_{i}}{\ldots}{\mu_{N}}}}_{{{q}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.74368pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.74368pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.26137pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.26137pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64622pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.80266pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu_{1}}{\ldots}{\hat{\mu}_{i}}{\ldots}{\mu_{N}}}}_{{{q}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.74368pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.74368pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.26137pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.26137pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][ (34c)

Here, the circumflex denotes an omitted index.
With this normalisation, transversality is ensured automatically when the reference vector is light-like.
For an already transverse tensor, the same condition reduces upon Fourier transformation to a harmonic differential equation, establishing a direct link to a spin-
N
polarisation tensor.

Symmetric, traceless tensors arise throughout physics, from multipole expansions in electrodynamics to gravitational-wave analysis (see Refs.
[
56
,
87
]
).
Our normalisation choice, however, differs from standard conventions as the one used in Ref.
[
88
]
.
Its purpose and motivation emerge from the explicit construction of a general harmonic tensor.

\displaystyle\mathchoice{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu_{1}}{\ldots}{\mu_{N}}}}_{{{q}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64622pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.80266pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu_{1}}{\ldots}{\mu_{N}}}}_{{{q}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64622pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.80266pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu_{1}}{\ldots}{\mu_{N}}}}_{{{q}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5 iptstyle$}}}}}\,,

(35)

where the sum runs over all permutations
\sigma
of the indices, modulo those that are equivalent either by the symmetry of the metric tensor or by the commutativity of products, and the corresponding coefficients are defined by

\displaystyle h^{N}_{2K}=(-1)^{K}2^{N}\frac{\Gamma\left(D-2\right)\Gamma\left(D/2-1+N-K\right)}{\Gamma\left(D-2+N\right)\Gamma\left(D/2-1\right)}\,.

(36)

This definition of the coefficients accounts for redundancies arising from the intrinsic symmetries of the metric tensor
\mathchoice{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}
and products of the reference vector
\mathchoice{q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}
.
Consequently, we sum only over permutations distinct under these equivalences, absorbing the combinatorial factors into the coefficients.

3.2
Harmonic Projection

To efficiently compute fixed Mellin moments of the coefficient functions, the method of projectors (see Refs.
[
50
,
51
]
) is used to project on individual terms in the operator product expansion.
The harmonic projection operator is defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{M}(\cdot)=\frac{1}{2^{M}}\frac{\mathchoice{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu_{1}}{\ldots}{\mu_{M}}}}_{{{q}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.22955pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.38599pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu_{1}}{\ldots}{\mu_{M}}}}_{{{q}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.22955pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.38599pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu_{1}}{\ldots}{\mu_{M}}}}_{{{q}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{ (37)

It has the property of yielding a non-zero result only when applied to a product of exactly
M
vectors
p
.
To see this, consider
M\neq N
and evaluate the action of

explicitly. Making use of the symmetry of the product and the defining properties of harmonic tensors, the indices can be relabelled as needed, leading to the derivative
