(38)

In the first case, the term vanishes trivially, since having more derivatives than factors reduces to a derivative of a constant, i.e. zero. In the second case, the projection vanishes as well, because at least one factor of
p
remains. Thus, the projection is non-zero only when
M=N
; in particular, one has

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{N}(\mathchoice{p^{{{\lambda_{1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.69307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.69307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.10834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.29167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\lambda_{1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.69307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.69307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.10834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.29167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\lambda_{1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.69307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.69307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.10834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.29167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\lambda_{1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.69307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.69307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.10834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.29167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\dots\mathchoice{p^{{{\lambda_{N}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.71431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.71431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55208pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.73541pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\lambda_{N}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.71431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.71431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55208pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.73541pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\lambda_{N}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.71431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.71431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55208pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.73541pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\lambda_{N}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.71431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.71431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55208pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.73541pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{M}}\mathchoice{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda_{1}}{\ldots}{\lambda_{N}}}}_{{{q}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.69307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.69307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.10834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.29167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.71431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.71431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55208pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.73541pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda_{1}}{\ldots}{\lambda_{N}}}}_{{{q}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.69307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.69307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.10834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.29167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.71431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.71431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55208pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.73541pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda_{1}}{\ldots}{\lambda_{N}}}}_{{{q}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.69307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.69307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.10834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.29167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.71431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.71431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55208pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.73541pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda_{1}}{\ldots}{\lambda_{N}}}}_{{{q}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.69307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.69307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.10834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.29167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.5pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.71431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.71431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55208pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.73541pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\,,

(39)

so that the result itself is a harmonic tensor.
Consequently, the harmonic projection applied to products of momenta
p
yields a harmonic tensor. In practice, the main interest lies in its application to the forward scattering amplitude. On the one hand, this amounts to applying it to the operator product expansion, i.e., to the hadronic invariants, where expressions of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{M}(T_{i})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{M}\sum_{k}A^{N}_{k,}(p)C^{N}_{i,k}(q)\Bigg|_{p=0}

(40)

arise.
Here it is used that by symmetry considerations the operator matrix elements depend only on
p^{2}
; thus, by the chain rule, derivatives acting on them do not reduce the total number of factors of
p
, so that all terms with derivatives acting on the operator matrix element vanish.

On the other hand, when evaluating the forward scattering amplitude through Feynman diagrams, the harmonic projection acts on scalar products involving the partonic momentum, especially those originating from the numerator structure of the Feynman rules. Further scalar products can appear in the denominators (see Ref.
[
94
]
), as these may be expanded in powers of
p
, for example:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(p+q)^{2}-m^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{q^{2}-m^{2}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{n}\left(\frac{2p\cdot q}{q^{2}-m^{2}}\right)^{n}\,.

In this case, the application of the harmonic projection is conceptually straightforward, as it amounts to differentiating the integrands and contracting harmonic tensors.

The expansion, however, introduces the first bottleneck of the method when higher moments are considered. By the Leibniz rule, the number of generated terms grows with the number of edges carrying the momentum
p
, so for practical calculations this number should be minimised.

3.3
Mass Factorisation

With all necessary ingredients assembled, the calculation can now be set up.
The harmonic projection is solely concerned with the
p
–dependence of the operator matrix elements, without regard to the specific processes they describe.
Moreover, it commutes with renormalisation, since the counterterms are local and therefore independent of
p
.
Consequently, the result obtained for the harmonic projection of the hadronic invariants carries over directly to the renormalised partonic invariants, ensuring that the projection can be performed prior to specifying the external states,

\displaystyle T_{i,\text{p}}^{\text{ren}}(p,q)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N}\sum_{j,k\in\set{a,\text{q},\text{g}}}C^{N}_{i,j}(q)Z_{jk}A^{N,\text{ren}}_{\text{p},k}(p)\bigg|_{p=0}\,,\qquad i\in\set{L,2},\text{p}\in\set{\text{g},\text{q}}\,.

(41)

Nullifying the partonic momentum affects only the operator matrix element.
In a theory with light flavours only, this matrix element would contribute solely at tree level after nullification of
p
, since all higher order terms in the coupling correspond to scaleless loop integrals, which vanish in dimensional regularisation.
The situation changes in the presence of heavy flavours as the heavy-quark mass provides a physical scale, giving rise to non-vanishing massive bubble integrals,

\displaystyle A^{N,\text{ren}}_{\text{p},k}(p)\bigg|_{p=0}

\displaystyle=A^{N,\text{ren},(0)}_{\text{p},k}(p)\bigg|_{p=0}+a_{s,\text{ren}}A^{N,\text{ren},(1)}_{\text{p},k}(p)\bigg|_{p=0}+a_{s,\text{ren}}^{2}A^{N,\text{ren},(2)}_{\text{p},k}(p)\bigg|_{p=0}

(42)

\displaystyle\qquad+a_{s,\text{ren}}^{3}A^{N,\text{ren},(3)}_{\text{p},k}(p)\bigg|_{p=0}+\mathcal{O}\left(a_{s,\text{ren}}^{4}\right)\,.

This issue is resolved by evaluating the operator matrix elements in a kinematic renormalisation scheme (see Ref.
[
38
]
) in which the finite contributions from the massive bubble integrals are removed through a finite renormalisation.
In such a scheme,

\displaystyle A^{N,\text{ren}}_{\text{p},k}(p)\bigg|_{p=0}

\displaystyle=A^{N,\text{ren},(0)}_{\text{p},k}(p)\bigg|_{p=0}\delta_{\text{p,k}}

(43)

\displaystyle\equiv\text{const}_{k}\delta_{\text{p,k}}\,,

which is constant with respect to the external momentum, though it still depends on
\epsilon
and the Mellin moment
N
.
The Kronecker delta arises because the off–diagonal operator matrix elements have no tree–level contributions and therefore vanish when the partonic momentum is set to zero.
Depicting the inserted operator vertex as a circle labelled by
N
, which denotes the spin of the operator, the operator matrix element may be represented graphically as

\displaystyle A^{N,\text{ren}}_{\text{q},\text{q}}(p)\Bigg|_{p=0}

\displaystyle=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{images/qqL0.pdf}}}\Bigg|_{p=0}+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{images/qqL1.pdf}}}\Bigg|_{p=0}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha_{s}^{2})

(44a)

\displaystyle\equiv k^{N}_{\text{q}}\,,

\displaystyle A^{N,\text{ren}}_{\text{q},\text{g}}(p)\Bigg|_{p=0}

\displaystyle=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{images/qgL1.pdf}}}\Bigg|_{p=0}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha_{s}^{2})

(44b)

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle A^{N,\text{ren}}_{\text{g},\text{g}}(p)\Bigg|_{p=0}

\displaystyle=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{images/ggL0.pdf}}}\Bigg|_{p=0}+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{images/ggL1.pdf}}}\Bigg|_{p=0}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha_{s}^{2})

(44c)

\displaystyle\equiv\text{k}^{N}_{\text{g}}\,,

\displaystyle A^{N,\text{ren}}_{\text{g},\text{q}}(p)\Bigg|_{p=0}

\displaystyle=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{images/gqL1.pdf}}}\Bigg|_{p=0}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha_{s}^{2})

(44d)

\displaystyle=0\,,

where the
k^{N}_{\text{p}}
are process dependent constants, i.e., they may depend on the regulator
\epsilon
due to the polarisation sum for the external gluons.
Substituting the operator matrix elements, one finds the renormalised partonic invariants

\displaystyle T_{i,\text{p}}^{N,\text{ren}}(q)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N}\sum_{j\in\set{a,\text{q},\text{g}}}C^{N}_{i,j}(q)Z_{j\text{p}}A^{N,\text{ren},(0)}_{\text{p},\text{p}}(p)\bigg|_{p=0}\,,\quad i\in\set{L,2}\,.

(45)

To extract the finite coefficient functions directly, assume that the coefficient function admits an expansion in the coupling and the regulator
\epsilon
, such that

\displaystyle C^{N}_{i,j}(q)

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}c^{N,(m,n)}_{i,\text{p}}(q)a_{s,\text{ren}}^{m}\epsilon^{n}\,.

(46)

Upon substituting the series expansions of the coefficient functions together with the
Z
–factors derived earlier, the normalisation is fixed at tree level,

\displaystyle T_{2,\text{q}}^{N,\text{ren}}(q)

\displaystyle=1+\mathcal{O}\left(a_{s,\text{ren}}\right)\,,

(47a)

\displaystyle T_{2,\text{g}}^{N,\text{ren}}(q)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(a_{s,\text{ren}}\right)\,,

(47b)

\displaystyle T_{L,\text{p}}^{N,\text{ren}}(q)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(a_{s,\text{ren}}\right),\qquad\text{p}\in\set{\text{q},\text{g}}\,.

(47c)

Note that this normalisation implicitly fixes the tree–level contributions to the moments of the coefficient functions.
Substituting the series expansions of the renormalisation constants and the coefficient functions, one finds

\displaystyle(-1)^{N}k^{N}_{\text{q}}T_{2,\text{q}}^{N,\text{ren}}=1+a_{s,\text{ren}}\Big\{\epsilon^{-1}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{q}\text{q}}+c^{N,(1,0)}_{2,\text{q}}+\epsilon c^{N,(1,0)}_{2,\text{q}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon^{2}\right)\Big\}

(48a)

\displaystyle\qquad+a_{s,\text{ren}}^{2}\Big\{\epsilon^{-2}\Big[\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{q}\text{g}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{g}\text{q}}+\big(\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{q}\text{q}}\big)^{2}\big)-\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{q}\text{q}}\beta_{0}\Big]+\epsilon^{-1}\Big[\tfrac{1}{2}\gamma^{N,(1)}_{\text{q}\text{q}}+c^{N,(1,0)}_{2,\text{q}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{q}\text{q}}\phantom{\Big[}

\displaystyle\qquad+c^{N,(1,0)}_{2,\text{g}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{g}\text{q}}\Big]+c^{N,(1,1)}_{2,\text{q}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{q}\text{q}}+c^{N,(2,0)}_{2,\text{q}}+c^{N,(1,1)}_{2,\text{g}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{g}\text{q}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon\right)\Big\}+\mathcal{O}\left(a_{s,\text{ren}}^{3}\right)

\displaystyle(-1)^{N}k^{N}_{\text{g}}T_{i,\text{g}}^{N,\text{ren}}=a_{s,\text{ren}}\Big\{\epsilon^{-1}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{q}\text{g}}+c^{N,(1,0)}_{2,\text{g}}+\epsilon c^{N,(1,0)}_{2,\text{g}}++\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon^{2}\right)\Big\}

(48b)

\displaystyle\qquad+a_{s,\text{ren}}^{2}\Big\{\epsilon^{-2}\Big[\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{g}\text{g}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{q}\text{g}}+\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{q}\text{g}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{q}\text{q}}-\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{q}\text{g}}\beta_{0}\big)+\epsilon^{-1}\Big[\tfrac{1}{2}\gamma^{N,(1)}_{\text{q}\text{g}}\phantom{\Big[}+c^{N,(1,0)}_{2,\text{g}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{g}\text{g}}

\displaystyle\qquad+c^{N,(1,0)}_{2,\text{q}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{q}\text{g}}\Big]+c^{N,(1,1)}_{2,\text{g}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{g}\text{g}}+c^{N,(2,0)}_{2,\text{g}}+c^{N,(1,1)}_{2,\text{q}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{q}\text{g}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon\right)\Big\}+\mathcal{O}\left(a_{s,\text{ren}}^{3}\right)

\displaystyle(-1)^{N}k^{N}_{\text{q}}T_{L,\text{q}}^{N,\text{ren}}=a_{s,\text{ren}}\Big\{c^{N,(1,0)}_{L,\text{q}}+\epsilon c^{N,(1,0)}_{L,\text{q}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon^{2}\right)\Big\}+a_{s,\text{ren}}^{2}\Big\{\epsilon^{-1}\Big[c^{N,(1,0)}_{L,\text{q}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{q}\text{q}}

(48c)

\displaystyle\qquad+c^{N,(1,0)}_{L,\text{g}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{g}\text{q}}\Big]+c^{N,(1,1)}_{L,\text{q}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{q}\text{q}}+c^{N,(2,0)}_{L,\text{q}}+c^{N,(1,1)}_{L,\text{g}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{g}\text{q}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon\right)\Big\}+\mathcal{O}\left(a_{s,\text{ren}}^{3}\right)

\displaystyle(-1)^{N}k^{N}_{\text{g}}T_{L,\text{g}}^{N,\text{ren}}=a_{s,\text{ren}}\Big\{c^{N,(1,0)}_{L,\text{g}}+\epsilon c^{N,(1,0)}_{L,\text{g}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon^{2}\right)\Big\}+a_{s,\text{ren}}^{2}\Big\{\epsilon^{-1}\Big[c^{N,(1,0)}_{L,\text{g}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{g}\text{g}}

(48d)

\displaystyle\qquad+c^{N,(1,0)}_{L,\text{q}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{q}\text{g}}\Big]+c^{N,(1,1)}_{L,\text{g}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{g}\text{g}}+c^{N,(2,0)}_{L,\text{g}}+c^{N,(1,1)}_{L,\text{q}}\gamma^{N,(0)}_{\text{q}\text{g}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon\right)\Big\}+\mathcal{O}\left(a_{s,\text{ren}}^{3}\right)\,.

The constants on the left-hand side are determined by the tree-level operator matrix elements, which are assumed to be normalised such that

\displaystyle k^{N}_{\text{q}}

\displaystyle=1\,,

(49a)

\displaystyle k^{N}_{\text{g}}

\displaystyle=(1-\epsilon)^{-1}\,.

(49b)

From this point, the finite part of the coefficient functions can be extracted recursively, once the left-hand side has been computed.

3.4
Details of the Calculation

When calculating the forward scattering amplitude, we sum over all gluon polarisations and light-quark spins and average over all colours.
For the polarisation sum, we could in principle employ a physical gauge with

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{\text{g}}}\mathchoice{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.26628pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.26628pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.61046pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.72176pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{s,\mu}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.26628pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.26628pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.61046pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.72176pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{s,\mu}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.26628pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.26628pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.61046pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.72176pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{s,\mu}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.26628pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.26628pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.61046pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.72176pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{s,\mu}}}}(p)\mathchoice{\epsilon^{{{*}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.07849pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34177pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{s,\nu}}}}{\epsilon^{{{*}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.07849pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34177pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{s,\nu}}}}{\epsilon^{{{*}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.07849pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34177pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{s,\nu}}}}{\epsilon^{{{*}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.07849pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34177pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{s,\nu}}}}(p)=-\mathchoice{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}+\frac{\mathchoice{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\mathchoice{q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}+\mathchoice{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}\mathchoice{q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}}{p\cdot q}-\frac{q^{2}\mathchoice{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}\mathchoice{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}}{(p\cdot q)^{2}}\,,

(50)

since
q
is not collinear to
p
.
Here,
\mathcal{S}_{\text{g}}
denotes the set of gluon polarisations and
\mathchoice{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.07849pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34177pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{s,\nu}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.07849pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34177pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{s,\nu}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.07849pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34177pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{s,\nu}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.07849pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34177pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{s,\nu}}}}
the polarisation tensor with respect to the momentum
p
.
If we employ the physical polarisation sum, the harmonic projection, being essentially a derivative operator, will generate numerous additional terms through extra factors of the momentum
p
.
Instead, for computational efficiency, we prefer an unphysical gauge, employing the polarisation sum

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{\text{g}}}\mathchoice{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.26628pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.26628pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.61046pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.72176pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{s,\mu}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.26628pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.26628pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.61046pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.72176pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{s,\mu}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.26628pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.26628pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.61046pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.72176pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{s,\mu}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.26628pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.26628pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.61046pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.72176pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{s,\mu}}}}(p)\mathchoice{\epsilon^{{{*}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.07849pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34177pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{s,\nu}}}}{\epsilon^{{{*}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.07849pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34177pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{s,\nu}}}}{\epsilon^{{{*}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.07849pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34177pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{s,\nu}}}}{\epsilon^{{{*}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.41324pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.07849pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34177pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{s,\nu}}}}(p)=-\mathchoice{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}\,.

(51)

This approach reduces the number of generated terms, but requires ghosts as external partonic states to compensate for the unphysical gluonic degrees of freedom.
Internal gluons are taken in a general
R_{\xi}
gauge, with gauge invariance ensuring that the gauge parameter
\xi
cancels in the final result, thereby providing a consistency check.

Using these conventions, the partonic invariants can be decomposed as

\displaystyle T_{i,\text{q}}(p,q)

\displaystyle=T^{\text{q}\text{v}\text{q}\text{v}}_{i}(p,q)\,,

\displaystyle i\in\set{L,2}

(52a)

\displaystyle T_{i,\text{g}}(p,q)

\displaystyle=T^{\text{g}\text{v}\text{g}\text{v}}_{i}(p,q)+2T^{\text{c}\text{v}\text{c}\text{v}}_{i}(p,q)\,,

\displaystyle i\in\set{L,2}\,.

(52b)

The factor of two for the ghost contributions is required, since both ghosts and anti-ghosts in the initial states must be summed over.
Superscripts denote the external particle content contributing to the forward scattering amplitude.

As defined previously, the projectors are not optimal for efficient calculation, as they are redundant in the sense that the longitudinal part is evaluated twice.
A further shortcoming is their dependence on the dimensional regulator
\epsilon
, which unnecessarily complicates intermediate steps of the calculation.
To address these issues, we decompose the projectors as

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{g^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\,,

(54a)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c] \displaystyle=4x^{2}\mathchoice{p^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{p^{{{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{p^{{{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\,.

(54b)

These projectors not only avoid redundant calculations but also allow a more efficient and modular implementation.
The reason is that they disentangle the Mellin moments, allowing the forward amplitude to be expanded to a fixed power of
p
rather than requiring term-by-term separation according to powers of the partonic momentum.

It should be noted that the flavour factors, namely the charges of the vector bosons, decouple from the amplitudes and appear as multiplicative factors.
This allows for the simultaneous calculation of the non-singlet and singlet contributions, as the amplitudes modulo the flavour factors are identical.
The flavour factors are fully determined by the open and closed fermion lines appearing in a graph and the vector bosons coupling to them.
Therefore, to define the factors, it is sufficient to consider only a single graph representing each such configuration.
This is illustrated in
Figure 1
, where, for completeness, all flavour factors that may appear, including those at three loops, are displayed for pure singlet configurations in the presence of a neutral current.
The concrete definitions are provided in
Appendix A
, since the flavour factors are not relevant for the subsequent discussion and become important only in phenomenological applications.

Figure 1
:
Collection of Feynman graphs showing examples for all flavour structures that appear in neutral current forward scattering amplitudes. Light quarks
l
are depicted in black, heavy quarks
h
in green, and vector bosons by
v
.

3.5
Feynman Graphs

The first step in computing the forward scattering amplitude is to generate all graphs contributing to the processes of interest, excluding tadpoles but including snails,

\displaystyle\text{p}+\text{v}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\text{p}+\text{v},

\displaystyle\quad\text{p}\in\set{\text{q},\text{g},\text{c}}

(55)

The graph generation was performed using the generator provided with
FORM
(see Refs.
[
59
,
44
]
),
and the results were checked independently with
QGRAF
(see Ref.
[
77
]
).
The so-generated graphs are not free of redundancies.
To remove them, it is advantageous to work with equivalence classes of graphs rather than individual graphs, declaring two graphs equivalent if their moments yield the same amplitude up to an overall sign.
These redundancies are eliminated in several stages by exploiting pattern matching and symmetry properties, following the procedures in
FORM
(see Refs.
[
92
,
63
,
82
,
90
]
).

As a preliminary step, all graphs that do not contribute to the amplitude must be identified, as they are all equivalent to zero.
The first such case involves graphs with vanishing colour factors.
For this purpose, it is advantageous to decompose all four gluon vertices, which separates the colour structures from the remaining numerator structures of the amplitude,

\displaystyle=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{images/4g1234.pdf}}}+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{images/4g1324.pdf}}}+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{images/4g1432.pdf}}}

(56)

Once all four-gluon vertices are decomposed into three-valent ones, the colour structure effectively decouples from the Lorentz structure of the amplitudes.
At this point, the Feynman rules can be assigned to the graphs, and the
FORM
package
color.h
(see Ref.
[
89
]
) can then be employed to compute all colour traces.

In the next step, other graphs contributing to the equivalence class of trivial graphs are determined.
This is done by identifying graphs that contain scaleless subgraphs after the harmonic projection is applied.
The reason is that the nullification of the partonic momentum effectively amputates the partonic edges whilst keeping the corresponding vertices.
The resulting graphs may then contain scaleless subgraphs upon identifying edges joined by a two-valent vertex that vanish in dimensional regularisation.
The criterion for identifying scaleless subgraphs is as follows.
After performing the harmonic projection, that is graphically the amputation of the partonic edges and contracting the edges joined by two-valent vertices, the resulting reduced graphs are examined.
They are analysed to determine whether they contain massless self-loops or are one-vertex reducible.
In the latter case, a graph that is not one-vertex reducible is considered scaleless if, upon the removal of a single vertex, it separates into more than one disjoint component, one of which contains neither external edges nor massive lines.
Another contribution to trivial graphs arises from cancellations due to charge conjugation, namely Furry’s theorem.
These graphs may be detected directly at the level of graph generation.

The final step is to relate graphs differing only by crossing external particles.
In forward scattering amplitudes, such a crossing corresponds to reversing the momenta
p
and
q
and, where relevant, exchanging indices between two particles of the same type.
To examine the effect of reversing the external momenta, consider a graph with
L
loops and only external scalar particles. Then, consider the forward scattering amplitude
\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}
with integrand
\mathcal{I}_{\Gamma}
, and project onto the
N
-th moment using the harmonic projection operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{N}(\mathcal{M}(p,q))

\displaystyle\equiv\int\differential[D]{k_1}\dots\int\differential[D]{k_L}\mathcal{P}_{N}(\mathcal{I}(p,q))

(57)

\displaystyle=\int\differential[D]{k_1}\dots\int\differential[D]{k_L}\sum_{M=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{P}_{N}\left(\mathchoice{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{1}}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{1}}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{1}}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{1}}}}}\dots\mathchoice{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.22955pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.38599pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{M}}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.22955pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.38599pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{M}}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.22955pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.38599pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{M}}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.22955pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.38599pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{M}}}}}\mathchoice{\hat{\mathcal{I}}^{{{\mu_{1}}{\dots}{\mu_{M}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\ \displaystyle=\int\differential[D]{k_1}\dots\int\differential[D]{k_L}\mathcal{P}_{N}\left(\mathchoice{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{1}}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{1}}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{1}}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{1}}}}}\dots\mathchoice{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64622pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.80266pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{N}}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64622pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.80266pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{N}}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64622pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.80266pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{N}}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64622pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.80266pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{N}}}}}\mathchoice{\hat{\mathcal{I}_{\Gamma}}^{{{\mu_{1}}{\dots}{\mu_{M}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.22955pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.38599pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hat{\mathcal{I}_{\Gamma}}^{{{\mu_{1}}{\dots}{\mu_{M}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.22955pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.38599pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hat{\mathcal{I}_{\Gamma}}^{{{\mu_{1}}{\dots}{\mu_{M}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.22955pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.38599pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hat{\mathcal{I}_{\Gamma}}^{{{\mu_{1}}{\dots}{\mu_{M}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.22955pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.38599pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(q)\right)\,.

By Lorentz symmetry,
\hat{\mathcal{I}}
can be constructed only from the vector
q
and the metric tensor
g^{\mu\nu}
, and must be totally symmetric to be compatible with contraction with a totally symmetric tensor.
Since the metric tensor carries two indices, tensors with an even number of indices must contain an even power of the momentum
q
, whereas those with an odd number of indices must contain an odd power.
Furthermore, all coefficients may only depend on
q^{2}
, so that

\displaystyle\int\differential[D]{k_1}\dots\int\differential[D]{k_L}\mathchoice{\hat{\mathcal{I}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.0625pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu_{1}}{\dots}{\mu_{M}}}}_{{{\Gamma}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.22955pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.38599pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hat{\mathcal{I}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.0625pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu_{1}}{\dots}{\mu_{M}}}}_{{{\Gamma}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.22955pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.38599pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hat{\mathcal{I}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.0625pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu_{1}}{\dots}{\mu_{M}}}}_{{{\Gamma}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.22955pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.38599pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hat{\mathcal{I}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.0625pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu_{1}}{\dots}{\mu_{M}}}}_{{{\Gamma}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.65022pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.22955pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.38599pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(q)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor\frac{N}{2}\rfloor}\hat{\mathcal{M}}^{N}_{\Gamma,2k}(q^{2})

(58)

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{\sigma/\sim}\mathchoice{g^{{{\sigma(\mu_{1})}{\sigma(\mu_{2})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.8135pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.08109pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.8135pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.08109pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\sigma(\mu_{1})}{\sigma(\mu_{2})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.8135pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.08109pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.8135pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.08109pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\sigma(\mu_{1})}{\sigma(\mu_{2})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.8135pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.08109pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.8135pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.08109pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\sigma(\mu_{1})}{\sigma(\mu_{2})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.8135pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.08109pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.8135pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.08109pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\ldots\mathchoice{g^{{{\sigma(\mu_{2k-1})}{\sigma(\mu_{2k})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[25.48482pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[25.48482pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[16.66245pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.93002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[21.40149pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[21.40149pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[13.74579pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[11.01337pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\sigma(\mu_{2k-1})}{\sigma(\mu_{2k})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[25.48482pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[25.48482pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[16.66245pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.93002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[21.40149pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[21.40149pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[13.74579pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[11.01337pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\sigma(\mu_{2k-1})}{\sigma(\mu_{2k})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[25.48482pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[25.48482pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[16.66245pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.93002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[21.40149pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[21.40149pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[13.74579pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[11.01337pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\sigma(\mu_{2k-1})}{\sigma(\mu_{2k})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[25.48482pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[25.48482pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[16.66245pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.93002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[21.40149pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[21.40149pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[13.74579pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[11.01337pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{q^{{{\sigma(\mu_{2k+1})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[27.6626pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[27.6626pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[18.21802pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[15.4856pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{q^{{{\sigma(\mu_{2k+1})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[27.6626pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[27.6626pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[18.21802pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[15.4856pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{q^{{{\sigma(\mu_{2k+1})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[27.6626pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[27.6626pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[18.21802pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[15.4856pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{q^{{{\sigma(\mu_{2k+1})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[27.6626pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[27.6626pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[18.21802pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[15.4856pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\ldots\mathchoice{q^{{{\sigma(\mu_{n})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.18744pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.18744pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.16432pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.4319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{q^{{{\sigma(\mu_{n})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.18744pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.18744pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.16432pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.4319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{q^{{{\sigma(\mu_{n})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.18744pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.18744pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.16432pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.4319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{q^{{{\sigma(\mu_{n})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.18744pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.18744pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.16432pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.4319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\,,

where the sum runs over all independent permutations
\sigma
of the indices, as described in the definition of harmonic tensors.
Hence, the
N
-th moment of the amplitude becomes

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{N}(\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}(p,q))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{N}\Bigg(\mathchoice{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{1}}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{1}}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{1}}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{1}}}}}\dots\mathchoice{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64622pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.80266pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{N}}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64622pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.80266pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{N}}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64622pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.80266pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{N}}}}}{p^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.83356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64622pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.80266pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu_{N}}}}}\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor\frac{N}{2}\rfloor}\hat{\mathcal{M}}^{N}_{\Gamma,2k}(q^{2})

(59)

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{\sigma/\sim}\mathchoice{g^{{{\sigma(\mu_{1})}{\sigma(\mu_{2})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.8135pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.08109pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.8135pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.08109pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\sigma(\mu_{1})}{\sigma(\mu_{2})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.8135pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.08109pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.8135pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.08109pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\sigma(\mu_{1})}{\sigma(\mu_{2})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.8135pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.08109pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.8135pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.08109pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\sigma(\mu_{1})}{\sigma(\mu_{2})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.8135pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.08109pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.69629pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.8135pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.08109pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\ldots\mathchoice{g^{{{\sigma(\mu_{2k-1})}{\sigma(\mu_{2k})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[25.48482pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[25.48482pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[16.66245pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.93002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[21.40149pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[21.40149pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[13.74579pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[11.01337pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\sigma(\mu_{2k-1})}{\sigma(\mu_{2k})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[25.48482pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[25.48482pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[16.66245pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.93002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[21.40149pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[21.40149pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[13.74579pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[11.01337pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\sigma(\mu_{2k-1})}{\sigma(\mu_{2k})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[25.48482pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[25.48482pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[16.66245pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.93002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[21.40149pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[21.40149pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[13.74579pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[11.01337pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\sigma(\mu_{2k-1})}{\sigma(\mu_{2k})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[25.48482pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[25.48482pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[16.66245pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.93002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[21.40149pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[21.40149pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[13.74579pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[11.01337pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{q^{{{\sigma(\mu_{2k+1})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[27.6626pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[27.6626pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[18.21802pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[15.4856pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{q^{{{\sigma(\mu_{2k+1})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[27.6626pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[27.6626pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[18.21802pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[15.4856pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{q^{{{\sigma(\mu_{2k+1})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[27.6626pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[27.6626pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[18.21802pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[15.4856pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{q^{{{\sigma(\mu_{2k+1})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[27.6626pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[27.6626pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[18.21802pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[15.4856pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\ldots\mathchoice{q^{{{\sigma(\mu_{n})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.18744pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.18744pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.16432pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.4319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{q^{{{\sigma(\mu_{n})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.18744pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.18744pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.16432pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.4319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{q^{{{\sigma(\mu_{n})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.18744pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.18744pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.16432pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.4319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{q^{{{\sigma(\mu_{n})}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.18744pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.18744pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.16432pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.4319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Bigg)\,.

Consequently, the amplitude is an odd function of either momentum for odd
N
and an even function for even
N
.
This property applies only to the Mellin moments, not to the amplitudes themselves, which in general are neither purely odd nor purely even functions of the momenta.

To extend this symmetry to individual graphs, one must note that the above argument applies only to graphs with external scalar edges.
Consider now the case where the external edges carrying momentum
p
correspond to partons.
For gluons, exchanging one gluon for another is always possible since they are their own antiparticles.
The polarisation sum is manifestly symmetric in the gluon indices, requiring that momenta and their associated indices to be swapped together.
External quarks and ghosts differ, as reversing quark lines is generally not possible, with similar restrictions applying to ghost lines.
These parity transformations are therefore admissible only for gluons, leading to the graphical identities,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{N}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{
\includegraphics[]{images/ggHmp.pdf}
}}}\right)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N}\mathcal{P}_{N}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{
\includegraphics[]{images/ggHpm.pdf}
}}}\right)=(-1)^{N}\mathcal{P}_{N}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{
\includegraphics[]{images/ggHpm.pdf}
}}}\right)=\mathcal{P}_{N}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{
\includegraphics[]{images/ggHmm.pdf}
}}}\right)\,.

(60)

If the external boson is taken to be a vector boson rather than a scalar, it is contracted with a symmetric tensor when projecting onto the partonic invariants.
Exchanging the bosonic momenta together with their indices therefore does not introduce an additional sign,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{N}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{
\includegraphics[]{images/ggSmp.pdf}
}}}\right)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N}\mathcal{P}_{N}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{
\includegraphics[]{images/ggSpm.pdf}
}}}\right)=(-1)^{N}\mathcal{P}_{N}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{
\includegraphics[]{images/ggSpm.pdf}
}}}\right)=\phantom{-}\mathcal{P}_{N}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{
\includegraphics[]{images/ggSmm.pdf}
}}}\right)\,,

(61)

where, for these symmetries to make sense, both vector bosons must be of the same particle type.

In the end, each diagram corresponds to an equivalence class.
To identify equivalences up to possible signs, the non-trivial graphs contributing to the calculation are matched against these equivalence classes, allowing for crossing of external particles.
Any signs arising in the process are accounted for directly.
Finally, the graphs are grouped into super-graphs (see Ref.
[
81
]
), defined as sums over all amplitudes with identical colour and flavour factors, up to overall numerical coefficients, that belong to the same integral family.
The numbers of graphs up to two loops for the processes considered in Eq. (
55
)
are listed in
Table 1
.

But it should be emphasised that the application of the symmetries is not crucial for the calculation of the fixed-order moments.
The reason is that dressing the graphs with Feynman rules is essentially constant and independent of the order of the moment to be calculated.
However, utilising the symmetries does nonetheless significantly improve the calculation at intermediate steps and provides a non-trivial self-consistency check, because the calculated amplitude should respect the symmetries.
